## Prayer for Faith.

->Tune: *Psalm 73 or 35.*<-

<margin>1.</margin> O precious promise,
Which is granted to us by God
In the precious blood of His Son,
Displayed for the sake of precious faith. <note>The repetition of "precious" (original: "dierbaar") mirrors the language of 2 Peter 1:1, "to those who have obtained like precious faith."</note>
Oh! truly, firmly built upon it,
Like stones, pearls, silver, gold.
*And therefore I pray, dear Lord,*
*Increase my small faith!* <note>original: "mijn klein geloove doch vermeer," referencing the prayer of the disciples in Luke 17:5.</note>

<margin>2.</margin> To stand in the day of evil,
The whole armor of God must be donned:
But above all, the Shield of faith:
Through which we turn back and extinguish
The arrows, which are so fiery,
From the old Serpent’s hellish venom. <note>A poetic rendering of Ephesians 6:11–16, describing the spiritual warfare of the believer.</note>
*And therefore I pray, dear Lord,*
*Increase my small faith!*

<margin>3.</margin> Satan sifts primarily
Faith, so that the heart might fail;
And for that very same cause
Has *Jesus*, our faithful advocate, <note>original: "voorspraak," a legal term for one who pleads a case on behalf of another.</note>
Prayed, that the heart-loyalty
Of our faith might not cease. <note>Refers to Luke 22:31-32, where Jesus tells Peter, "Satan has asked to sift all of you as wheat. But I have prayed for you, Simon, that your faith may not fail."</note>
*And therefore I pray, dear Lord,*
*Increase my small faith!*

<summary>This page concludes a hymn celebrating Christ's total sufficiency and begins a poetic prayer for the strengthening of faith, drawing heavily on biblical imagery of spiritual armor and Christ’s intercession.</summary>
<keywords>righteousness, David Montanus, All in All, armor of God, Shield of Faith, spiritual warfare, intercession of Christ, Satan's sifting</keywords>

--- Page 44 ---
<meta>This page continues the "Prayer for Faith" (Gebedt om ’t Geloove) started on the previous page. The poem concludes with a series of metaphors describing the vital necessity of faith in the life of a believer, ending with the signature of the author, David Montanus.</meta>

4. Faith alone has the power
To put the flesh to death with its strength; <note>To "mortify the flesh" is a common theological concept referring to the subduing of worldly desires.</note>
The world lies entirely in wickedness,
Among the earthly-minded and the godless.
But through a Christian-believing heart,
All this alone is overcome.
*And therefore I pray, dear Lord,*
*Increase my small faith!* <note>original: "mijn klein geloove doch vermeer," a recurring refrain based on the apostles' request in Luke 17:5.</note>

5. What the soul is to the body,
And oil is to the wagon wheel;
And the largest gear to smaller wheels,
The root to the stem and leaves,
The sail to the ship, the wind to the sail—
Such is faith to the soul’s salvation;
*And therefore I pray, dear Lord,*
*Increase my small faith!*

6. O hand, with which I grasp and touch!
O tongue, with which I taste;
O feet, with which I walk;
O firm foundation of love and hope!
O plaster for my painful wound, <note>In the 17th century, a "plaster" (original: "plaiſter") was a medical dressing or ointment applied to heal a wound.</note>
O strengthening of my fainting heart!
*And therefore I pray, dear Lord,*
*Increase my small faith!*

7. O eye that captures the heart
Of Him who heals both soul and body!
Who, in order to crown faith,
Stripped Himself of His own honor; <note>This refers to the theological concept of "kenosis" or Christ's self-emptying, where God humbles Himself to reach humanity.</note>
Because he who believes in the Son,
Crowns his head through self-denial.
*And therefore I pray, dear Lord,*
*Increase my small faith!*

8. He who is righteous before the Lord,
Shall, according to His promise,
Live by his faith alone; <note>A reference to the biblical verse: "The just shall live by faith" (Habakkuk 2:4; Romans 1:17).</note>
Which must give spirit to his spirits,
Yes, gives blessing to him and all his own,
When he lives through his faith.
*And therefore I pray, dear Lord,*
*Increase my small faith!*

->D. MONTANUS<- <note>David Montanus (1621–1686), a Dutch Reformed minister and poet associated with the Pietist movement.</note>

<meta>This page marks the beginning of a new treatise titled "Christ, the Way, the Truth, and the Life." # CHRIST,
## The Way, the Truth, and the Life.

->Or<-

### A discovery of the proper way to use CHRIST for Sanctification. <note>In this context, "Sanctification" (original: "Heyligmaking") refers to the process of becoming more holy or more like Christ through the work of the Holy Spirit.</note>

->From<-

### JOHN 14:6.

> *Jesus said to him: I am the Way, the Truth, and the Life; No one comes to the Father, except through me.*

---

## CHAPTER I.
### The Introduction, with some general observations from the context. <note>The "context" (original: "aan-een-hang") refers to how these specific verses relate to the passages surrounding them in the Bible.</note>

<image-desc>Decorative initial 'H' featuring elaborate foliate designs within a square border.</image-desc>

IT is undoubtedly always useful, indeed necessary, for the children of God to know the proper way to make use of Christ, who has become everything to them that they need; namely wisdom, <term>righteousness</term> <note>The state of being right or justified before God.</note>, <term>sanctification</term>, and <term>redemption</term> <note>Deliverance from sin and its consequences.</note>: 1 Corinthians 1:30. But it is never more necessary for believers to know how to act clearly and discerningly in this matter than when <term>Satan</term> seeks by all means to pervert the straight ways of the Lord, and in one way or another to lead souls away—

<sig>A</sig>
<meta>catchword: from</meta>

<summary>This page serves as the title and introduction to a theological treatise based on John 14:6, emphasizing the practical necessity of "using" Christ for spiritual growth and sanctification. It frames the study as a defense against spiritual deception.</summary>
<keywords>Christ, The Way, Sanctification, Righteousness, Redemption, John 14:6, 1 Corinthians 1:30, Spiritual Warfare</keywords>

--- Page 46 ---
<meta>This page continues the introduction from page 1, completing the sentence about Satan's efforts to lead souls away from Christ. It establishes the central conflict of the treatise: the temptation for believers to rely on their own morality or "cleverness" rather than on the spiritual life provided by Jesus.</meta>

# Christus the Way,
<meta>catchword: from</meta>

from and to draw [them] away from *Christ*, knowing full well that if he prevails here, he has won his case. And there he seeks not only to obscure the Truth through error <note>original: "dwaling"</note>, be it coarser or finer, but also to obscure it through misconceptions and prejudices; from which it happens that not only strangers come to wander off from the Way, but also frequently many of God’s own People come to walk in darkness of ignorance and misconceptions, and remain meager for lack of the actual exercise of the Life of Faith. This exercise would otherwise make them healthy and flourishing, because it would make them strong in the Lord, and in the strength of His power, and would cause them to grow up in *Christ* in all things.

The laying open then of this Truth can be nothing other than very timely now, when *Satan* prevails over many—those whom he cannot entice to looseness and profanity—to sit down upon something that is not *Christ*, and to rest upon something that is within themselves, which is distinct from Him, both in the matter of <term>justification</term> <note>Being declared righteous before God.</note> and of <term>sanctification</term> <note>The process of growing in holiness.</note>. This cunning adversary now sets some to work to proclaim, to preach, to speak, and to write of a thing as a Way to Heaven which is not *Christ*. It is a sort of morality, civility, and outward holiness upon which the soul would rest; and this is yet a holiness that is not wrought and brought about through the power of *Jesus* by faith sucking life and nourishment from Him, but through our own art and cleverness. In deed, this is nothing other than an extract of subtler <term>Popery</term> <note>A term used by reformers to refer to Roman Catholic doctrines of merit.</note>, <term>Socinianism</term> <note>A rationalist theology that denied the full divinity of Christ and emphasized his moral example.</note>, and <term>Arminianism</term> <note>A theological movement that emphasized human free will in the process of salvation, often viewed with suspicion by the Dutch Reformed Church.</note>, expressly devised and brought forth to pull the Soul away from *Christ*, so that it might stand on its own legs, walk by its own strength, and attribute the end to itself—or at least in part—giving itself the credit for receiving the crown.

# the Truth and the Life.
A 2
<meta>catchword: noge</meta>

...efforts; <note>original: "pent", likely completing a word such as "pogingen" (efforts) or "hulpmiddelen" (means) from the previous page</note> and many have—indeed in a contemplative way <note>original: "beschouwender wÿze"; an intellectual or theoretical understanding as opposed to a lived experience.</note>—knowledge of this; and many also know it in practice, for the reassuring of their awakened <term>consciences</term> <note>original: "Conscientien"; the inner sense of right and wrong, often discussed in this period as being 'awakened' by God to recognize sin.</note> and the stopping of the mouths of their accusers, and for the obtaining of peace, joy, and the living hope of the eternal crown. Nevertheless, many a graced soul confesses her ignorance regarding the steady and prosperous way of using *Christ* for growth in grace and true <term>sanctification</term> <note>original: "heyligmaking"; the process of being made holy and more like Christ in character.</note>. Therefore, some uncovering of the truth here can be nothing other than useful, timely, and pleasant to her.

If He, who is the *Truth*, would grant grace to understand and lay open this so necessary and always profitable truth, and would help in writing about this truth and explaining it through faith in Him, who is sent here to be the *Truth*, then we would have cause to praise and magnify His Name. But if He, on account of sin, should hide Himself and not send forth these rays of light through which we might discover light, then we shall only darken counsel with words without knowledge, and leave the matter as unclear as it ever was.

Therefore it is necessary that there be—both in him who writes and in those who read—a simple dependence upon Him who is given as a Leader (Isaiah 55: verse 4) and has promised to lead the blind by the Way they have not known, and to make them walk through the paths they have not known, and to make the darkness before their face into light, and the crooked into straight (Isaiah 42: verse 16). So that we, thus exercising faith in Him, might find the truth of this verified—namely, that He is the *Way*, the *Truth*, and the *Life*.

Now, to lay this matter clearly open, we should know that our Lord *Jesus*, from the beginning of this chapter, lays down some grounds of comfort which are sufficient to console them against the sad news of His departure and Death, and to encourage them against the fear they had that much evil would come upon them when their Lord and Master would be taken from them; which a satisfac-

# Christ the Way,

<warning>Ink stain on top left corner.</warning>

This is sufficient proof of the tender heart of **Jesus**, who gives all His followers strong comfort against all fears, dangers, difficulties, and anxieties that they may encounter on their way. He will not leave them comfortless; and therefore He lays down strong foundations of comfort to support their pining and failing hearts, as He gladly sees His followers always *rejoicing in the Lord* and singing in the *ways of Zion*, so that the World may see and be convinced of the reality of Christianity. He wishes them to see that this life—notwithstanding all the difficulties that attend it—is to be preferred above all other kinds of life, however sweet and desirable it may seem to **Flesh and Blood**.

<margin>verse 4, 3.</margin>
In pursuing this aim, He told them that they knew where He was going, and knew the path that He Himself would take, as it was the one through which He would bring them to the Father, to the dwellings that had been spoken of, and so to eternal **Life**. But **Thomas** burst out rashly and unbelievingly (as he commonly did; see John 11: verse 16 and John 20: verse 25) and did little less than contradict his Master, saying (verse 5), *We do not know where You are going, and how can we know the way?* In this, we have a reflection of many a believer, who may possess more grace and knowledge of **God** and **Christ** than they are able to see or acknowledge that they have. This happens either through temptation or internal imbalances; through a feeling of their many failings and great ignorance; through strong desires for a higher measure of grace; through clearer revelations of the majesty <note>original: "wijstheidt," likely meaning the vastness or excellence of the divine subject</note> of the object; through misunderstandings about the true nature of grace; through "despising the day of small things" <note>A reference to Zechariah 4:10, meaning to undervalue small beginnings or modest spiritual progress</note>; through a lack of clear understanding of the workings of grace; or by a lack of insight and a proper conception of grace in its various outcomes and operations under various names, etc.

<meta>This page continues the discourse on Christ as the "Way," following the previous discussion where the author noted how Christ comforts His followers. # The Truth and the Life.

[He] takes <note>the opportunity</note> to further explain that foundation of comfort to them, and to make them see the true way to come to the Father, so that they might be helped by it to see that they were not such strangers to the way as they thought they were. At the same time, He expands upon and presents the characteristics and excellences of this way, as being the true and living Way, and the only true and living Way. He does this in such a manner that they might simultaneously see that the Way is perfect, full, safe, saving, and sufficient, and also learn their duty to use this way always and in everything, until they finally come home to the Father, saying:

> *I am the Way, the Truth, and the Life: No one comes to the Father except through me.*

When *Christ* says then, that He is not only the Way to the Father—namely the true and living Way—but that He is so much the true Way that He is also the Truth itself, in the abstract <note>original: "in het afgetrokkene"; meaning in His very essence or absolute nature</note>, and that He is such a living Way that He is the Life itself in the abstract, He gives us a foundation to notice in what manner He is the Truth and the Life as well as the Way. This serves to clarify and reveal that He is an absolutely perfect, surpassingly excellent, incomparably desirable, and fully satisfying Way—useful for the believer in all cases, in all needs, in all miseries, in all difficulties, in all trials, in all temptations, in all doubts, in all anxieties, and in all causes or occasions of spiritual imbalances <note>original: "ongetempertheden"; refers to a lack of inner peace or emotional/spiritual instability</note>, fears, faintings, discouragements, etc., which they might encounter on their way to Heaven. 

And this shall lead us, on the other hand, to point out the duty of believers, and to show how they shall use *Christ* in all their various cases and difficulties, as the only all-sufficient way to the Father, and as Truth and Life upon the Way. And so we shall be led to speak of how *Christ* is everything to His people that is required for them here on the way, whether for justification, whether for...

<meta>This page continues the exposition of John 14:6, where Jesus declares Himself "the Way, the Truth, and the Life." This page transitions into discussing how Christ serves as our sanctification and how He responds to the doubts and weaknesses of His followers, specifically using the Apostle Thomas as an example.</meta>

->Christ the Way,<-
<meta>catchword: his</meta>

sanctification, and how the people must make use of him as being everything, or as having been made by God for us into wisdom, righteousness, sanctification, and redemption. <note>original: "wijsheidt / gerechtigheidt / heyligmakinge / en verlossing"; a reference to 1 Corinthians 1:30.</note>

Before we come to the words specifically, let us consider them as they relate to the words of Thomas in the preceding verse, in which he did little less than contradict what Christ had said in the 4th verse. From this, we shall learn several very comforting points of doctrine, namely:

Firstly: That Jesus Christ is very tender toward those who follow him, and that he will not reject them, nor reproach them for every fault by which they provoke him and grieve his Spirit. Instead, he kindly passes over many failings when he finds that they are not obstinate in their misunderstanding, nor perverse in their way. For how kindly and gently he here passes over the fact that Thomas does little better than contradict him, finding that Thomas spoke here not out of obstinacy and stubbornness, but out of ignorance and a misunderstanding. And the reason is: 1. Because Christ knows our weakness and fragility, and is of a tender heart, and therefore will not break the bruised reed, Isaiah 42: verse 3. He well knows that harsh and hard handling would crush us and break us entirely to pieces. And 2. He is full of the depths of mercy <note>original: "ingewanden van barmhertigheidt," literally "bowels of mercy," a common 17th-century idiom for deep compassion</note>, and knows how to have compassion on those who are off the path, and can be touched with the feeling of our infirmities, Heb. 4: verse 15 and Heb. 5: verse 2.

Which truth, just as it should on the one hand encourage all to choose him as their leader and to surrender themselves to him who is so tender over those who follow him; so it should also, on the other hand, serve to rebuke those who are inclined to maintain evil and hard thoughts of him, as if he were a hard Master and difficult to follow. And it should keep all people from holding the slightest thought about—

--- Page 51 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of Christ’s compassionate nature, specifically how He responds to the doubts of His disciples. The previous page ended mid-sentence, describing Christ as being full of the depths of mercy.</meta>

-># the Truth, and the Life.<-

his lack of tenderness and compassion; but further.

Secondly, we see that weaknesses and corruptions <note>The Dutch "verdorventheden" refers to the inherent flaws or "corruptions" of human nature in a theological sense.</note> breaking out in the believer, when they are sincerely and simply laid before the Lord, will not drive Him away, but will bind Him all the more to help and stand by them. Much of the weakness and corruption of *Thomas* appeared in what he said; nevertheless, when these were sincerely and simply placed before *Christ*—not out of a spirit of contradiction, but out of a desire to learn—*Christ* is so far from pushing him away that He much more inclines Himself, out of love and tenderness of heart, to instruct him better and to lay open the way more perfectly. And that for two reasons: 1. Because He well knows our frame and fashion, how insignificant and fragile we are, and that if He were to deal with us according to our folly, we would quickly be destroyed. 2. He is not as *men* are—hasty, frivolous, or proud—but kind, loving, tender, and full of compassion. It is His office and His own work to be a teacher of the ignorant, a helper of our weaknesses and fragilities, and a *Physician* <note>original: "Medicijnmeester," literally a "Medicine-master" or master healer.</note> to bind up and heal our sores and wounds.

<margin>*</margin> Who, then, would not willingly surrender themselves to such a *Teacher*, who does not want to cast out sinners, nor give them up to themselves whenever their corruptions might provoke Him to do so? And what folly it is in many that they stand far off from *Christ* because of their weaknesses, and are frightened away from Him because of their powerlessness, whereas the more corruption we find in ourselves, the more we should run toward Him? It is time enough to go away from *Christ* when He pushes us away and says that He will have no more to do with us; indeed, He wishes to allow us to remain standing even after we have been, as it were, cast out three times. Only let us take care that we do not justify ourselves in our corruptions, that we do not hide them as if unwilling to part from them, that we do not stubbornly maintain them, nor establish ourselves in them; but that we

His revelations and expressions of grace and love are diverse. Small beginnings can finally amount to much. Thomas and the other Disciples had but little clear and distinct perception of the way of salvation through Jesus Christ, and yet before all was finished, they obtained a great measure of understanding in the mysteries of God, so that we are said to be built upon the **Foundation of the Apostles**, Jesus Christ being the **chief cornerstone**. # Christ the Way,

and what that way is, and it answers the objection that *Thomas* put forward; namely, that because He was the Way, etc., and they, having knowledge of Him (which is presented here beforehand), were not ignorant of the place where He was going, nor of the way that led there. The knowledge of *Jesus Christ* is therefore the true summary of all saving knowledge; for this reason, it is eternal life to know Him, *John 17:3.* Those who know Him, know the *Father*, *John 14:7* and *John 8:19* <note>The original text cites John 11:19, but this is likely a misprint in the original Dutch for John 8:19, which matches the theological context of knowing the Father.</note>. Those who see Him, also see the *Father*, *John 14:9.* He is in the *Father*, and the *Father* in *Him*, *John 14:10, 11*, *John 10:38*, *John 17:21.* And so by knowing Him, they knew *Heaven*: for what else is *Heaven* than the presence and glorious revelation of the *Father*? For when *Christ* speaks of His going to *Heaven*, He says that He went to the *Father*. Thus knowing Him, they knew the way: how *Christ* as our *Surety* <term>Surety: a legal and theological term for one who takes responsibility for another's debt; in this case, Christ taking on the debt of human sin.</term>, *Head*, and Advocate would go to *Heaven*, and how we must follow.

Let a person have ever so much knowledge and understanding of all the arts and sciences, the depths of nature, the secrets of state, and all the contemplation of *Religion*; if he has no knowledge of *Christ Jesus*, then he knows nothing as he ought to know.

And on the other hand, let a poor soul who is sincere and has but a single knowledge and understanding of Him be content, even if they cannot argue or dispute, nor speak of cases of *Conscience* <term>Cases of Conscience: complex moral or spiritual dilemmas that were a major focus of 17th-century "puritan" or "precisianist" thought.</term> like some others can. If we know Him, it matters little if we are ignorant of many things for which we are held in lower esteem by others. Here is the true guideline by which we can take a proper measure of our own or others' knowledge. The true rule by which to test knowledge is not subtle concepts or clear and distinct expressions, but heart-knowledge of Him in whom all the treasures of wisdom and knowledge are hidden, *Colossians 2:3.*

Oh, how sad it is! That we are not more absorbed in this study, which would be the nearest way for us to

### Concerning the words themselves in general.

<image-desc>Large ornamental woodcut initial 'W' decorated with scrolling foliage and floral patterns within a square frame.</image-desc>

We come now to the words themselves; in which Christ establishes that he *1. is the way, 2. the truth, 3. the life,* and 4 that no one comes to the Father but through him. From this we learn these two things in *general*.

First: The misery of the wretched <note>original: "catifvigen," related to the word 'caitiff,' meaning a miserable, captive, or base person.</note> human being by nature. This cannot be expressed in few words. These words show these particularities thereof, which we shall only recount:

1. That he is a born enemy of God, and living far from God, by virtue of the curse of a broken covenant of life, made with Adam. <note>The "Covenant of Life" (or Covenant of Works) refers to the theological belief that God promised life to Adam on the condition of perfect obedience, which was lost at the Fall.</note>

3. That he is a blind wandering creature, inclined to take by-ways and to stray; indeed, he loves to wander off; he goes astray from his mother's womb, speaking lies.

## Chapter III.

# How Christ is the Way, in general:

->I am the Way.<-

<image-desc>Large decorative woodcut initial N featuring a complex pattern of vines, flowers, and foliage within a square frame.</image-desc>Now we come / to speak more specifically of these words: And first, regarding how he is a Way. Since our aim is / to show the way to use Christ in all our needs / distresses / and difficulties / that are upon our path to Heaven / and in particular to demonstrate the manner / in which believers shall use Christ in all their specific needs / and thus through faith live in Him / walk in Him / grow up in Him / progress in Him / and proceed toward glory / so it shall not be in vain / to seek this fullness of Christ for the unbelieving / as the opportunity shall present itself; because this shall help to clarify the other even more.

<meta>This page (20) continues the discussion from page 19 regarding humanity's inability to find the true spiritual path on their own. Having established that Christ is the exclusive "Way," the author now begins to categorize the "false ways" or self-deceptions that people mistakenly trust for their salvation.</meta>

Christ the Way,

as if they could get onto that way of their own accord? What a lamentable spectacle it is to see how people weary themselves—even with lies, *Ezekiel 24: verse 12*—and how each wanders their own way! *Isaiah 47: verse 15.*

But what are these false and deceitful ways in which people exhaust themselves, all in vain? Which ways do they choose and trust in, even though they are not the way that will be found safe and secure?

I answer: it would not be easy to list them all. We shall mention some of the most prominent and common ones, namely:

1. **Good intentions and resolutions**, with which many deceive themselves, thinking that such is all that is required. Alas, all their intentions are like Ephraim’s goodness: like the morning cloud and the early dew that vanishes quickly. <note>The author references Hosea 6:4, comparing fleeting faith to morning mist.</note> Their intentions are soon broken off and nullified because they are made without counsel, *Proverbs 15: verse 22.* Many foolishly rest in the fact that they have a "good mind" to do better and to improve their ways; they plan that after this or that time, they will begin a new way of life. But their intentions never come to any effect, and so they and their intentions eventually perish.

2. **Certain convictions and internal accusations**; the Word <note>The "Word" refers to the Bible or the preaching of Scripture.</note> occasionally pierces so deep, and heavy and sharp visitations <note>A "visitation" from the Lord usually refers to a period of divine testing, judgment, or trial intended to wake the conscience.</note> from the Lord affect their hearts so much that they see things are not right with them. They must cry out with Saul: 
> I have sinned,
*1 Samuel 15: verse 24.* Yet they progress no further; either these convictions die away again, or they produce no further change. And those poor souls imagine—because they had such convictions and sharp accusations during such a sermon or such a Lord's Supper <note>original: "Avontmaal," the Dutch term for the sacrament of Holy Communion.</note>—that all is then well with them. So they delude themselves, though a Judas can have sharper convictions than they ever had; as could also Felix, *Acts 24: verse 25.*

# the Truth, and the Life.

...[fear]ful thing to be deceived here; and it is best to put it to the test while it is still possible to have the matter rectified. Many may come to fear and tremble when they see that they have not yet come as far as many who still sit without Christ and lose all their labor. Oh, that this might stir people to an earnest inquiry and testing of themselves, and of the nature of that way in which they are, and upon which they currently rest!

Thirdly. We may note here that this true and living way is but one. There is but one Mediator <term>Mediator: a theological title for Jesus, signifying his role as the bridge or go-between for humanity and God</term> between God and man, *1 Timothy 2: v. 5*, one Mediator for the Old and New Testament; the "seed of the woman." <note>original: "’t zaadt der Vrouwe." This refers to the prophecy in Genesis 3:15, which Reformed theologians viewed as the first promise of Christ.</note> Although the Lord’s dispensations <term>dispensations: the different ways or systems by which God has dealt with humanity throughout history, such as the era of the Old Testament Law versus the era of the New Testament Gospel</term> with His people on that one way may be diverse: just as His manner of dealing with His people under the Law is different from His manner of dealing with His people under the Gospel; and His dealings with particular believers, whether under the Law or under the Gospel, are not the same in everything.

And this should teach us to abandon our own ways and to come onto this one and only way; and it should move those who are on this way to strive for unity and agreement among one another. Nevertheless, they should not conclude nor think that God’s way with them must be the same in all things as His way with others; even if the Lord's way with them is different—being darker, more comfortless, and more bitter—let them still be peaceful and keep silent before the Lord, and acknowledge His goodness, for He has brought them onto that one and only way, Jesus Christ, and keeps them there.

But fourthly. The principal matter, and that which immediately presents itself to us here, is this: that Jesus Christ is the way to the Father, the one and only way, the supreme and excellent way, and He alone is that way; there is no other. Nor is there salvation in any other: for there is no other name under Heaven gi-

--- Page 72 ---
<meta>This page transitions from the general necessity of a Mediator to a specific four-part examination of Christ as "the Way." It begins a new section focusing on why humanity requires such a path and how Christ uniquely fulfills that need, picking up from the broader discussion of theological dispensations on the previous page.</meta>

# Christ the Way,

given among men, whereby we must be saved. <note>Acts 4: v. 12.</note>

To explain this clearly, we shall speak briefly of these four things and show:

**First, what our state is, and how much we need a way.**

**Secondly, how *Christ* answers this state of ours, and is a suitable way for us.**

**Thirdly, how He alone is this way, and satisfies our case.**

**Fourthly, what the uncommon advantages and particulars of this way are.**

And this shall pave the way, so that we may clearly show how *Christ* must be used as a path by poor sinners.

Regarding the first matter, namely our present case and need, something has been spoken of this previously. We shall bring everything under these two headings. The first is our state of guilt and separation from God, due to sin and guilt. The other is our state of godlessness and enmity against God.

Regarding the first, we may take notice of these things:

1. That original and actual sin <term>actual sin: individual sins committed by a person in their lifetime, distinguished from "original sin" which is the inherited fallen nature from Adam</term> has separated us from God, and cast us out of His favor, and thrust us from that state of favor and friendship to which we were once exalted in *Adam*.

2. That we are under God’s curse and wrath, and banished from the presence of the Lord, by a sorrowful yet just sentence, according to the Law; and so we are under Death.

Regarding the other, we may take note of these particulars:

1. That we are unclean and defiled with sin and daily unrighteousness.

2. That we are ignorant of the right way to return to the favor of God, seeking many inventions <note>original: "veele vonden." A reference to Ecclesiastes 7:29, implying that humans create their own complicated and ineffective ways to reach God instead of following His path.</note> for ourselves.

<meta>catchword: 3. That</meta>

<meta>This page continues the numbered list from the previous page, detailing the fallen state of humanity before explaining how Christ serves as the "Way" to resolve these specific spiritual crises. It transitions from human helplessness to Christ’s restorative work.</meta>

### the Truth, and the Life.

3. That we are powerless to perform any good deed or any commanded duty.

4. That we are not only thus powerless, but also unwilling to do anything that is good, or to enter upon the way when it is shown to us; indeed, we are enemies of God through evil works, and have an innate hatred against all His ways.

5. We do not desire to leave that state in which we find ourselves; there we lie, and we gladly sleep, desiring neither to be aroused nor awakened.

6. We are under the power and the command of Satan, who leads us off the path, even driving us forward on the wrong way to our destruction.

These things are clear and cannot be denied; they require no further confirmation, although they are, alas, believed by few and taken to heart by even fewer.

**Secondly.** Regarding how *Christ* answers this state and need of ours: He is a way for us, to help us out of both—out of our state of guilt and separation, and out of our state of godlessness and enmity.

**Firstly.** He helps us out of our state of guilt and separation.

1. By taking away our guilt and sin, having become sin for us though He knew no sin, so that we might become the righteousness of God in Him. <note>2 Corinthians 5:21</note> He has filled the great breach <note>original: "bresse"</note> between God and us with His body, and has made of it, as it were, a bridge over which we might go to the Father; we now enter into the Sanctuary <note>original: "Heyligdom," referring to the Holy of Holies in the Temple</note> through the blood of Jesus, by a new and living way which He has consecrated for us through the Veil, that is to say, through His flesh. <note>Hebrews 10:19, 20</note> We are now brought near by His blood, <note>Ephesians 2:13</note> so that through Him we are restored again to friendship with God, and have become one with Him: For Christ the Mediator <term>Mediator: a go-between who reconciles two parties, in this case, God and humanity</term> has made both one,

### Christ the Way,

that the soul / who once enters upon it / is safe for eternity; here one does not go astray. The traveling people / even the foolish, shall not wander on this way, <note>original: "Iesa."; an abbreviation for Jesaja, the Dutch name for the prophet Isaiah</note> Isaiah 35 verse 8. He will lead the blind by the way they have not known; he will cause them to tread by the paths they have not known; he will make the darkness light before their face, and the crooked straight; these things he will do for them, and he will not forsake them, Isaiah 42: verse 16.

5. This way is also **the Life**; and so it can revive the fainting and weary traveler. He gives strength to the weary, and increases the power of those who have no strength; yes, he renews their strength, and makes them rise up with wings, like eagles; he makes them run, and not grow weary, walk, and not grow faint, Isaiah 40: verses 29, 31. Oh, who would not want to walk upon this way! What can make a person discouraged / who walks here? What can he fear? No ordinary road can revive and refresh a weary person; this way can do it. Indeed, it can bring to life one who is as if dead, and make him proceed with fresh alertness and <term>vigor</term> <note>original: "vigueur"; the author uses this French loanword to emphasize a renewed, dynamic energy of the spirit</note>.

6. From all this it follows / that this Way is a most pleasant / joyful / desirable / and comforting way. A person is safe here / and he may sing in the ways of the Lord / Psalm 138: verse 5. For the ways of wisdom are ways of pleasantness / and all her paths are peace / Proverbs 3: verse 17.

...works; if we could only have Heaven through the way of works / we would spare no effort / no cost / no labor or expense / yes, we would even subject ourselves to many pains and torments / through floggings / cuttings / fastings / vigils / and the like; we would not spare our firstborn; yes, we would dig our graves with our own nails in a rock / and shorten our days / if we could only obtain Heaven by these means; such is our resistance against the way of salvation <note>original: "zaligheidt"</note> through a crucified Christ / that we would choose all ways / rather than this one / whatever it might cost: Therefore, before we can heartily embrace Christ / and receive Him / we must be weaned from all these refuges of lies / and see that nothing is written upon them all / but a deception in expectation / and that all our prayers / fastings / cries / duties / improvements / suffering / good wishes / good deeds / etc. are nothing in His eyes / but only so many provocations in the eyes of His jealousy / and thus further causes of our miseries.

Therefore, the poor weak believer need not become so discouraged / that he would despair / and give up the case as hopeless and lost; let faith cling / depend / and wait; that which is a weak faith today / can become stronger in a short time; He who laid the foundation / can and will complete the building: for all His works are perfect; and a weak faith / when it is true / will be soul-saving / and it will take hold of a soul-saving strong **Mediator.** <term>A "Mediator" (Middelaar) refers to Jesus Christ acting as the legal and spiritual representative of humanity before God.</term>

Furthermore, concerning the operation of faith upon **Christ's** <note>original: "Christi"</note> death / and sacrifice / to silence the **Conscience** / the **Law** / and **Satan** / and to withstand the justice of **God** / which pursues on account of sin / sometimes its operation can be strong / distinct / clear / and resolute; at other times it can be weak / mixed / or accompanied by much fear / dizziness / doubt / and distrust / because one sees one's own unworthiness / many failings / doubts about the sincerity of their conversion and the like.

This is a primary matter / and of great importance; yet many are not much disturbed by it / nor have their hearts as engaged in it as they ought / deceiving themselves with vain thoughts.

**For.**

1. They imagine that they were believers all their days / never did they doubt **God's** grace and good favor / they always had a good heart toward **God** / although they never knew / what an awakened **Conscience** <term>An "awakened conscience" is a theological term for a person becoming painfully aware of their own sin and the necessity of divine forgiveness.</term> / or a feeling of **God's** wrath meant.

### **unto Justification.**

I answer. 1. That we have such an absolute need of him is a basis / to urge us / that we should go / and seek help and rescue; we see / that we are lost in ourselves / and therefore it is open to us / to go and seek help elsewhere.

2. **Christ’s** all-sufficient fullness / by which he is a well-provided **Mediator** <note>A "Mediator" is a go-between; here it refers to Christ acting as the bridge between a holy God and sinful humanity.</note> / provided with all necessities for our case and state / having brought a price to satisfy justice / is a sufficient invitation for us / to look to him for help / and to wait at his door.

2. <note>The number '2' is repeated in the original text instead of '3'.</note> That he is appointed by the **Father** / to be the **Mediator** of the **Covenant** / and in particular / to lay down his life / as a ransom for sin / and that **Christ** has taken up all his offices / and has performed all the duties of the same / in accordance with the **Covenant** of **Redemption** <note>A theological term for the eternal agreement between God the Father and God the Son regarding the salvation of mankind.</note> ; such is a strong encouragement for poor sinners / to come to him / because he cannot deny himself / and he will be faithful in carrying out his charge.

4. That the **Father** offers him to us in the **Gospel** / and that he himself invites us / who are weary and heavy laden / yes that he calls such / and commands them to come to him in his own / and in his **Father’s** name / under the penalty of his and his **Father’s** wrath / and eternal disfavor; that he further awakens / and requests on conditions of love / through many motives earnestly urges / sends out his Ambassadors <note>Referring to ministers or preachers who deliver the message of the Gospel.</note> / to pray the poor sinners on his behalf / that they would be reconciled / and turn to him / to have life and salvation; yes that he reproaches those / who do not want to come to him / all this is a sufficient ground / for a poor deficient sinner / to grasp this offer.

And further / to encourage the poor souls / to come to him; everything is so well arranged in the **Gospel** / that nothing appears there / that can be in the least a stumbling block / or a righteous ground of

They cannot object to the greatness of their sins: for the greater their sins are / the more they have need of one / who is sent / to take the sin away / and whose blood cleanses from all sin <note>original: *1 Ioh. 1: vers 7.*</note> **1 John 1: verse 7.** What great sinner did he ever refuse / who came to him / and was willing to be saved by him? [...walk] in his ways / as well as a **Priest** to satisfy justice for us / to die and to pray for us. Is it not reasonable / that we accept him just as God has made him for us? **Thirdly**, It is required of us / that we accept him for nothing / without money / and without price / <note>original: *Iesa. 55 vers 1.*</note> **Isaiah 55 verse 1**. For he will in no way be bought, so that free grace may be free grace / therefore he will give all for nothing. It is certain enough / that our corrupt nature <note>original: *verdorbenheidt*; referring to the theological concept of total depravity or the fallen state of humanity.</note> would gladly buy / even though it has nothing to give; pride will not bow / to receive a free gift; but can anyone say / the conditions are heavy / when everything is offered for nothing?

**Fourthly**, It is required of us / that we shall accept him absolutely / without any limitation or reservation <note>original: *sins-behoudenis*; a "mental reservation" where one outwardly agrees but inwardly keeps a condition.</note>; some would indeed be willing to leave all except one or two lusts <term>lusts: in this 17th-century context, "lusts" refers to any illicit desires or sinful attachments, not solely sexual ones.</term> to leave behind / from which they do not think / they can part; and they would willingly deny themselves many things / but they would always very gladly keep a back door open / for one or another beloved lust. And who does not see / what a double-heartedness is here? And what reason can plead for this deceitful dealing? He who loves any known lust / and would not gladly be released from it / has no actual hatred against any lust / as such.

# Christ the Way,

act of the soul seizing Him / and grasping Him / just as He is offered in the Gospel / many shall be justified. verse 11. <note>original: "vers 11"; likely referring to Isaiah 53:11: "by his knowledge shall my righteous servant justify many."</note> For this reason He says / that all whom the Father has given Him / shall come to Him / **John 6: verse 37.** And the Apostle tells us / that we are blessed with all spiritual blessings in Him. **Ephesians 1: verse 3.**

### unto Justification. ###

to revoke the consent that she has given to the purchase or the contract; but always to look forward, to persevere, to wrestle against unbelief and unwillingness, to hold onto every good movement of the Spirit <note>original: "Geeſt"; referring to the Holy Spirit's influence on the heart.</note> for this purpose, and never to allow anything that could extinguish her longings, desires, or expectations.

**Tenthly**, If the soul has come so far that she—with that small amount of willingness she possesses—consents to the transaction, and is not satisfied with anything in herself that pulls away or does not consent; and if she, with that little skill or strength she has, sets her name down there, saying, "Yes, indeed, I accept Him," and holds fast to that, being absolutely resolved never to turn back or deny what she has said, but on the contrary, she is firmly minded to cling to Him, and as she grows in strength, to grasp more firmly and seize Him—then she may conclude that the contract is already closed, and that she already possesses faith. For here is an acceptance of Christ on His own terms <note>original: "conditien"</note>, an actual consenting to the covenant of grace <note>In 17th-century theology, the "Covenant of Grace" is the promise of God to be the God of His people through the mediation of Christ.</note>, although weak and not as discernable as the soul would wish. The soul dares to say nothing else but that she loves the contract, is satisfied with it, longs for it, and desires nothing more than to have a part in it and to enjoy Him whom she loves, hungers for, and pants or sighs after—to the extent she is able—so that she may live in Him and be saved through Him.

But some will say, "If I had some evidence of God's approval of this act of my soul, some testimony from His Spirit, then I could say with confidence that I had believed and had accepted the covenant with Christ offered therein. But as long as I do not perceive this, how can I maintain that any movement of this kind in my soul is actual faith?"

To this I answer: First, we must know that our believing and God's sealing <note>original: "verzegelen"; a reference to the 'sealing' mentioned in Ephesians 1:13, where the Holy Spirit confirms the believer's standing.</note> are two distinct, separable, and often separated acts—

# Christ the Way, #

to be; our faith is one thing, and God’s sealing with the Holy Spirit of promise is another thing; and the one follows—though not inseparably—the other, Ephesians 1, verse 13: *In whom you also, after you believed, were sealed with the Holy Spirit of promise.*

**Secondly.** Thus we also ought to know that many a soul can believe, and yet not know that she believes; she can set her seal to this, that God is true in His offering of life through Jesus, and accept that offering as a truth, and embrace it, and yet live under darkness, through doubts of her faith, for many a day and a length of time; partly because she does not discern the true nature of faith; partly through the intense feeling and noticing of her own corruption and unbelief; partly through a misunderstanding of the workings of the Spirit within, or through the lack of a clear and distinct perception of the movements of her own soul; partly because she finds so much doubt and fear, as if there could be no faith where doubt or fear is—which is directly contrary to Mark 9: verse 24, Matthew 8: verse 26, and Matthew 14: verse 31; <note>These biblical references point to instances where "little faith" or faith mixed with doubt is still recognized by Jesus as valid faith.</note> partly because she has not had the assurance <note>original: "versekertheydt"; refers to the internal confidence or "certainty" of one's own state of salvation.</note> that others have had, as if there were not various degrees <term>trappen</term> <note>Literally "steps" or "stairs," a common 17th-century theological term indicating that faith grows in stages or levels of strength rather than being the same for everyone.</note> of faith, just as there are of other graces, and such like.

Therefore, **thirdly**, we ought to know that many can actually believe, and yet miss this felt <note>original: "gevoel-lijke"; meaning a sealing that can be perceived by the senses or emotions.</note> sealing of the Spirit which they so dearly desire. God may not yet judge it timely to give her that, lest she should forget herself and become proud, and to further nurture her in the exercise of faith, whereby He might be glorified.

**Fourthly.** Nevertheless, we ought to know that those who believe have the seal within them, 1 John 5: verse 10: *He who believes in the Son of God has the witness...*

<meta>This page continues the transition from the topic of justification (being made right with God) to sanctification (growing in holiness), referencing William Guthrie’s influential work mentioned on the previous page.</meta>

# Christ the Way,

### treatise—namely, *The Christian’s Great Interest*; 

so shall we come and show how a believer, or a justified soul, shall further use *Christ* for sanctification <note>The lifelong process of becoming more holy and Christ-like in character and behavior.</note>, since this is a particular matter regarding which she <note>The "soul" is traditionally referred to with feminine pronouns in this period's theological writing.</note> is often very perplexed and burdened.

In order that we might, to some extent through the help of His light and grace, present some things to clear up this great and necessary truth, we shall first speak of it in general, and then proceed to show the matter more particularly. Before we speak of the matter in general, we ought to remember that the *Person* who alone is in a state to use *Christ* for sanctification is one who has already used *Christ* for righteousness and justification <note>The legal act where God declares a sinner righteous through faith in Christ.</note>. He who is a stranger to *Christ* and still lives in a natural state has no access to *Christ* for sanctification. He must be a believer and be in the covenant <note>Referring to the Covenant of Grace, a core Reformed theology concept describing God's promise of eternal life through faith.</note> before he can make use of the grounds of sanctification presented in the covenant; one must first be united with *Christ* and justified through faith in him before one can draw any power from him for the perfecting of holiness. He must first be in him before he can grow in him, or bring forth fruit in him. And therefore, the first thing with which souls should occupy themselves is how to be united with *Christ* and clothed with his righteousness through faith; and then they have a right to all his benefits. First, they should seek to have their state changed from enmity to peace and reconciliation with *God* through faith in *Jesus*.

But on the other hand, one should note that when it is said that one must be a believer before one can go to *Christ* and use him for holiness and purification, it is not understood nor said that one must *know* for certain that they are already justified through faith before they can make any use of *Christ* for sanctification.

# On Sanctification in General

...to be sanctified by God the Father, Jude verse 1. The Son is also called the sanctifier / Hebrews 2: verse 11. He sanctifies and cleanses the congregation with the washing of water by the word, Ephesians 5: verse 26. The Spirit is also said to sanctify us / 2 Thessalonians 2: verse 13, 1 Peter 1: verse 2, Romans 15: verse 16. For this reason we are said to be washed and sanctified by the Spirit of God / 1 Corinthians 6: verse 11. But more particularly, we are said to be sanctified in Christ, 1 Corinthians 1: verse 2, and he has become to us from God for sanctification <note>original: "tot heiligmaking"</note>, 1 Corinthians 1: verse 30. Let us then see / in what sense this may be true.

Firstly: He has brought about through his Death and Blood / that this work of sanctification shall be wrought and advanced: for he suffered outside the gate, so that he might sanctify the people through his own Blood: Hebrews 13: verse 12. We are saved through the washing of rebirth <note>original: "wedergeboorte"</note>, and renewal of the Holy Spirit, which he has poured out abundantly upon us through Jesus Christ, our Savior, Titus 3: verses 5, 6. He gave himself for us, so that he might deliver us from all unrighteousness, and cleanse for himself a people for his own possession, zealous for good works, Titus 2: verse 14. Thus our sanctification is the fruit of his Death / and is obtained through his Blood / He gave himself for his Church, so that he might sanctify her, Ephesians 5: verses 25, 26.

Secondly: Since he died as a surety <note>A "surety" is a legal guarantor who takes responsibility for another person's debt or obligations.</note> and public person / so believers are legally reckoned to have died to sin / in him. This Sanctification is an article of the Covenant of Redemption** <note>The Covenant of Redemption (original: "Verbondt der Verlossinge") refers to the eternal agreement between God the Father and God the Son regarding the salvation of the elect.</note> **between the Father and the Son** / Isaiah 52: verse 15. *So shall he sprinkle many **Gentiles**,* and Isaiah 53: verse 10. *He shall see his seed, and the pleasure of the Lord shall prosper in his hand.* Since this then is promised to **Christ** / he must see it fulfilled / and it shall also be given to him / since he had fulfilled all / that he had undertaken / having made his soul a **guilt offering**.

**Sixthly. This Sanctification is promised in the Covenant of Grace** <note>The Covenant of Grace (original: "Verbond der Genade") is the theological framework for God's offer of salvation to humanity through faith in Christ.</note> / Jeremiah 33: verse 8. *And I will cleanse them from all their iniquity,* Ezekiel 37: v. 23. *and I will cleanse them;* so also Ezekiel 36: verse 25. *Then will I sprinkle clean water upon you, and you shall be clean from all your filthiness, and from all your idols* <note>original: "drekgoden," literally "dung-gods," a derogatory term used in the Dutch States Translation to emphasize the filth of idolatry.</note> *I will cleanse you.* Now all the promises of the **Covenant of Grace** are confirmed to us in the **Mediator**: for in him all the promises are **Yes and Amen.** 2 Corinthians 1: 20.

**Seventhly: He has also obtained / and secured for his own the new nature / and the heart of flesh / which is also promised** / Ezekiel 36: verse 26. and Ezekiel 11: verse 19. Jeremiah 32: verse 19. This is the new and living principle of grace / the fountain of **sanctification** / which cannot be in vain or silent in the soul.

**Eighthly. Yes, through him believers become partakers of the Divine nature / which is a growing thing / a young glory in the soul.** 2 Peter 1: verse 3. 4. *According as his divine power has given unto us all things that pertain unto life and godliness,* <note>original: "Godzaligheyd"</note> *through the knowledge of him that has called us to glory and virtue, whereby are given unto us exceeding great and precious promises, that by these you might be partakers of the **divine nature**, etc.*

**Thirdly**, They ought to guard themselves against a careless neglect of the means / established for progress in holiness: for although the means do not accomplish the work / yet it is through the means that God has chosen to perform the work of sanctification; here it is seen / that the hand of the diligent makes rich / and the field of the sluggard is soon overgrown with thorns and nettles / so that poverty comes upon him / like a traveler / and lack as an armed man / **Proverbs 24: 30, 31, 34.** It is a sinful tempting of God / to think / that one shall be sanctified in another way / than God in His deep wisdom has appointed.

**Fourthly**, They should nonetheless guard against placing too much weight upon the means and ordinances <term>ordinances: established religious practices like prayer, reading Scripture, or the sacraments</term> / as if they could bring about the work themselves. Although it has seemed good to the Lord / to work in / and through the means; yet He himself must do the work. Means are but means / and not the principal cause; and they cannot work otherwise / than as the principal worker pleases to use them / and to work through them: If we lean on the means / and on the instruments / then we disadvantage ourselves / by giving God displeasure / and provoking Him / to leave us / so that we struggle with the ordinance alone / and find no benefit. Therefore the soul should arm itself against this.

**Fifthly**, Although the means can do nothing / unless He breathes upon them <note>original: *over-ademe*; a metaphor for the Holy Spirit providing the "breath of life" to religious activities</note>; yet we should not only guard against neglecting them (as we have just said) / but also against observing them in a sloppy manner / without that earnestness and diligence / which is required. *Cursed is he, who does the work of the Lord negligently.* **Jeremiah 48: verse 10.** Here then the particular art of Christianity is evident / that one is so diligent / earnest / and focused in the use of the means

**Sixthly**, They ought to guard themselves against treating the stirrings of the Spirit lightly / or neglecting them: for through this they may lose the best opportunity. When the stirrings of this indwelling Spirit are quenched / then His work is spoiled and hindered: and when He is grieved / then He is obstructed in His work. Therefore souls should watch out against unbelief / despondency / unbecoming and unchristian behavior / etc.

**In the next place**, It would be useful / and very beneficial for the soul / that desires to grow in grace / and advance in the way of holiness / if it lived in constant conviction.

# Christ the Way,

thus to cast himself, and his care and burden upon Him who cares for him. <note>1 Peter 5:7, Psalm 37:5, Psalm 55:22</note> In this way the work would become light and certain, as we through faith roll the burden upon Him who is the Chosen One, made fit for that work; and leave it to Him, who is our strength, patiently awaiting the outcome in hope.

In this manner the believer makes use of Christ, as having been made by God for sanctification, <note>A reference to 1 Corinthians 1:30: "Christ Jesus, who of God is made unto us wisdom, and righteousness, and sanctification."</note> when in the use of the established means—looking to the Covenant of grace and its promises, and to what Christ has done to sanctify and purify His people—he rolls the matter upon Him and expects help, salvation, and victory through Him.

But lest some should become faint-hearted, and think that all is in vain because they nevertheless perceive no progress or growth in grace, but find corruption to be as strong and troublesome as ever, I wish to say a few things to them.

**First.** Let them examine and test whether their shortcoming and failure in expectation does not largely arise because the matter was not cast so purely upon Christ as it ought to have been. Is it not too often found that they go out to battle in their own strength, leaning on their own capital <note>original: "capitaal"; referring to one's own internal stock or accumulated reserves of spiritual strength</note> of grace, on their own knowledge, or on their duties, or the like? How then can they be successful?

**Secondly.** Let them mourn when they receive any discovery of this, and arm themselves hereafter against that corrupt inclination of the heart which continually prompts them to engage in the battle without the Captain <note>original: "Oversten"; can also mean Chief, Prince, or Commander</note> of their salvation, and to fight without the armor of God.

**Thirdly.** Let them test and see whether, in striving for holiness, they are not led by wrong ends, and whether they do not seek sanctification more so that they may be worthier and better accepted by God,

<meta>catchword: be /</meta>

<summary>This page advises believers to rely entirely on Christ for sanctification rather than their own strength or "spiritual capital." It warns against the legalistic trap of seeking holiness merely to earn God's acceptance.</summary>

### On Sanctification in General

...be; and so that they might have rest and peace regarding their adoption and reconciliation with God, as if this were some reason, substance, or condition for their righteousness and justification <note>In Reformed theology, justification is the legal declaration of being righteous before God, distinct from sanctification, which is the process of becoming holy.</note> before God; but rather, that they might show their obedience to the command of God (1 Thessalonians 4:3; Ephesians 2:10; John 15:16) and express their gratitude to Him, and glorify God (Malachi 1:6; Matthew 5:16; John 17:10; Ephesians 4:30). And if this is the case, then they ought to acknowledge God’s goodness in this failure of their expectations, since they see through it more and more a necessity to lay aside their own righteousness, and to betake themselves to the righteousness of Christ, and to lean on that alone for peace and reconciliation with God.

**Fourthly.** They should examine and see whether their neglect and carelessness in watching <note>Referring to the biblical concept of spiritual watchfulness—remaining alert against sin and temptation.</note> and in the execution of duties does not cause them to miss their expectations and thus fall short. God sometimes deems it fitting to allow a season of spiritual dullness <note>original: "verdooventheydt"; a state of being numbed, senseless, or in a spiritual stupor.</note> to come over them, so that they may look back and stand more alertly on their watch-tower, knowing that they have to deal with an alert adversary, the Devil, who goes about like a roaring lion, seeking whom he might devour (1 Peter 5:8). And that they have a struggle not only against flesh and blood, but against the principalities, against the powers, against the rulers of the darkness of this world, against the spiritual wickedness in the air (Ephesians 6:12). It is not in vain that we are so often commanded to watch (Matthew 24:42; Matthew 25:13; Matthew 26:41; Mark 14:38; Luke 21:36; Mark 13:33, 34, 35, 37; 1 Corinthians 16:13; 1 Thessalonians 5:6; 1 Peter 4:7; Colossians 4:2). We know what befell David and Peter through a lack of watchfulness.

<term>corruption</term> <note>original: "verdorventheyd"; the inherent inclination toward sin that remains in a person even after religious conversion.</note> can stir and cause much commotion / in places where it is not permitted to rule; it can be like a powerful and cruel <term>usurper</term> <note>One who seizes power or a throne illegally.</note> / seeking the throne / setting the whole kingdom on fire / while being resisted by the force of arms; corruption can be more peaceful and quiet / when it actually possesses the throne of the soul; just as a conqueror can be more peaceful and quiet / when he has conquered / and is in peaceful possession of the kingdom / than when he was merely fighting for it; when the strong man occupies his house / and is master / then everything is in rest and peace / until a stronger one comes / and casts him out / and drives him from his possession. <note>A reference to the parable of the "strong man" found in Matthew 12:29 and Luke 11:21-22.</note>

### CHAPTER VI.

## How *Christ* is to be used, in respect of the killing and crucifying of the Old Man.

<image-desc>A large decorative woodcut initial letter 'H' featuring floral and vine motifs within a rectangular frame.</image-desc>

Having thus briefly pointed out some things in general / serving to explain and open the way / how *Christ* is to be used for <term>sanctification</term> <note>original: "heilighmakingh"; the process of being made holy.</note> / we now come / to lay this work open more particularly. In sanctification we must first observe the renewal and change of our nature / and form. Secondly, the washing and cleansing of our daily acquired stains. The first of these is commonly divided into two parts, namely the killing, <term>mortification</term> <note>original: "mortificatie"; from the Latin 'mortificatio', referring to the spiritual practice of "putting to death" sinful desires.</note> / and crucifying of the <term>old man</term> <note>original: "ouden mensch"; a biblical term for the human nature corrupted by sin.</note> of sin / and of the corruption / that is within us; and the <term>quickening</term> <note>original: "levendigmaking"; bringing to life.</note>, renewal / awakening / and strengthening of the <term>new man</term> <note>original: "nieuwen mensch"; the nature of a believer transformed by grace.</note> of grace; And this is a growth in grace / and in fruitfulness / and holiness.

As concerns the first of these / namely / the Mortification, or crucifying of the Old Man / we ought to know / that there is now / since the <term>Fall</term> <note>original: "val"; the theological event of humanity's first sin in Eden.</note> / such a principle of wickedness and enmity against God in man / through which man is inclined to evil / and only toward evil. This is called the Old Man / being as it were a body / made up of so many parts / joints / and members / that is / so many lusts / and corruptions / and evil inclinations / which together make up a *body* <note>original: "corpus"</note> / and are firmly united / and bound together / just like the members of the body / each useful and of service to the other / and all these working together / and contributing their best

It describes the inherent corruption of human nature since the Fall as a cohesive "body" of sinful lusts and inclinations.</summary>

bringing forth [the means] to carry out the work of sin; and so it is the man of sin, and is called the **old man**, as having first possession of the graced soul, and which dies daily more and more. Thus it is called the **old man**, and the **body of sin**, Romans 6: verse 6. This old man has his **members** <note>In 17th-century theology, "members" refers not just to body parts but to the various faculties of the soul, like the will and intellect.</note> in our members and faculties, so that none of those are free; the **understanding**, **will**, **affections**, and the members of our body are all **servants of unrighteousness** to this old man and to this body of sin. So we read of the *motions of sin*, Romans 7: verse 5, which work in our members to bear fruit unto death, and of the *lusts of the flesh*, Romans 13: verse 14; Galatians 5: verses 16, 24; and of the *lusts of sin*, Romans 6: verse 12. So we hear of the *desires of the flesh* and of the *mind* [thoughts], Ephesians 2: 3, and of the *passions and lusts*, Galatians 5: 24. And the old man is said to be corrupt through the *deceitful lusts* [lusts of seduction]: Ephesians 4: verse 22. All which lusts and motions are as so many members of this body of sin, and of this old man. And further, herein is remarkable a power, violence, and strength, which this old man has in his lusts, to carry us away, and as it were to command or compel us, as by a powerful law. Therefore we read of the *law of sin and of death*, Romans 7: verse 2, from which only the **law of the Spirit of life in Christ** makes us free. It is also called a *law in our members*, warring against the law of our mind, Romans 7: verse 23. Thus it is said to desire to war against the Spirit, Galatians 5: 17, all of which points to the strength, activity, and dominion of sin in the soul, so that it is as the husband over the wife, Romans 7: verse 1: yes, it has an overmastering and compelling power, where its <term>horns</term> <note>original: "hoornen"; a biblical metaphor (often from the Psalms or Revelation) representing power, pride, or aggressive strength.</note> are not restrained by grace. And as its strength is great, so its nature is godless and wicked: for it is pure enmity against God, Romans 8: 7.

<header>to the killing of Sin</header>

so that it is not reconciled, nor can it be reconciled; and
therefore it must be laid aside and brought to nothing,
Ephesians 2: verse 15, killed and crucified,
Romans 6: verse 6. Now herein lies the work of a believer:
that one kills, mortifies <term>mortification: the spiritual practice of draining the power of sinful impulses</term>, and crucifies this enemy, or
rather this enmity, and delivers oneself from this
servitude and slavery, so that one may be Christ’s freedman,
and that through the Spirit.

Now if one asks: how shall a believer use Christ
in order to get this old man crucified? Or how should a believer 
kill the old man and its desires through Christ,
or through the Spirit of Jesus? We shall present these 
things which can help to explain this. Namely:

First. The believer ought to keep his eye upon
this old man as his arch-enemy <note>original: "arts-vyand"</note>, as a deadly
murderer lying within his bosom. It is
an enemy lodging within him—in his soul, mind,
and heart and affections—so that no part is free;
and therefore he has knowledge of all the movements of the
soul, and always resists and hinders everything that is good.
It is an enemy that will never be reconciled with God,
and therefore he will not be reconciled with the believer 
as such; for he is called enmity 
itself, and so he is always active, seeking to 
further the ruin of the soul, both by inciting 
to evil, inclining, moving, and powerfully pulling 
or driving (sometimes with violence and fury), 
as well as by resisting, opposing, standing against, 
counter-working, and speaking against that which is good; 
so that the believer cannot get done that which he 
would do, and he is brought to do that 
which he would not. Since this is such an enemy then, 
and so dangerous, so constant and irreconcilable an 
enemy, so active and intimate <note>original: "innige"</note> an enemy, so deadly and 
destructive; so it is the duty of the believer to 
guard himself against this enemy, to have a watchful eye 
upon it, and to behave as an irreconcilable enemy 
against it.

against; and therefore never to come to terms of capitulation or agreement with the same; never even to parley, let alone make peace with it. And the believer ought not to have his watchful eye so much on this or that limb of this body of death, as on the body itself, or on the principle of godlessness and rebelliousness <term>rebelliousness: the active resistance of the human will against God's commands</term> against God—the Head, the Life, the Spirit, or the Law of this body of death: for therein lies its greatest malice and activity; and this always opposes us, though not in every part and limb [at once], but sometimes in one, sometimes in another.

Secondly. Although the believer ought to have a primary eye upon the body—upon this innate, strong, and violent law of sin and of death—yet he must have no friendship or intimacy with any part, limb, or lust of this entire body; all the workings of the body ought to be killed, Romans 8: verse 13. The old man <note>The "old man" is a biblical term referring to the unregenerate human nature before spiritual transformation.</note> with his deeds ought to be put to death, Colossians 3: verse 9, and we must kill our members which are on the earth, verse 5: for all these are against us, and the least of them favored, entertained, and embraced will work our ruin and murder our soul; therefore the believer ought to look upon each one of them, and upon them all, as his deadly enemies.

Thirdly. He ought to consider that just as it is a very unseemly thing for him to be a slave of that old Tyrant, and to present his members as so many servants of unrighteousness; it is also dangerous and deadly; his life depends on it; either he must get sin mortified <term>mortified: from the Latin 'mortificatio', meaning the putting to death or subduing of sinful desires through spiritual discipline</term>, killed, and brought under subjection, or it will kill him: his life shall have to go for the life of sin; if this enemy escapes, he is a lost man. The consideration of this ought to make the believer set to work here with earnestness and diligence, with care and industriousness, and to undertake this work of mortification with labor and effort.

### ->Towards the killing of sin.<-

Fourthly. It must be much more contrary to all reason and **Christianity** for the believer to care for the flesh <note>The "flesh" (Greek: *sarx*) refers to the fallen human nature that remains in the believer, driving them toward selfish or sinful desires.</note> and make provision for it, to fulfill its lusts, Romans 13: verse 14. That one should strengthen the hands of this enemy and provide for him—who is set and sworn against us—cannot stand with any reason. And here much of the Christian’s **prudence** and **spiritual wisdom** is required to discern what may serve to nourish this or that corruption, or member of the body of sin and death, and to withdraw it; just as we seek to take away all provisions from an enemy who comes against us. Paul acted in this as a wise wrestler and fighter when he kept his body under and brought it into subjection, 1 Corinthians 9: verse 27. It would be but a mocking of God and a revelation of our own foolishness if one looked up to Christ for help against such an enemy while, in the meantime, one strengthened the enemy's hands; this would be a **double-heartedness** <term>double-heartedness: a state of hypocrisy or divided loyalty where a person claims to serve God while secretly indulging sin</term> and a treachery.

Fifthly. In order that their opposition against this enemy might be stronger and more resolute, they ought to note that this body of sin is entirely set against God and against His interest in the soul, being indeed actual enmity against God, Romans 8: verse 7, and always desiring and fighting against the work of God in the soul, Galatians 5: verse 17, and against all that is good. So much so, that it will not permit (as far as it can hinder it) the soul to do anything that is good—at least not in a right manner and for a right end. Indeed, through its desiring, it constantly drives toward that which is evil; it stirs up evil motions and inclinations in the soul before she is even aware of it; it takes the side of any temptation that is offered, to the end that it might ruin the soul, like a **traitor** who is within. Just as we see it did in **David**, when he fell into adultery, and in **Asaph**, Psalm 73: verse 2. Indeed, it opposes itself and it tempts, James 1:14, through the understanding, the will, and the inclinations toward a perverse...

And besides this craftiness and cleverness, sin gives power and strength to cause one to sink into destruction and ruin through its lusts, *1 Timothy 6: verse 9.* and to thrust the soul down from above, so that it makes the state of **Man** miserable, *Romans 7: verse 24.*

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the believer's spiritual struggle, moving from the repair of the "Armor of God" to the source of victory: Christ’s death on the cross. It focuses on the legal and spiritual liberation from the "body of death" and the "law of sin."</meta>

-> ### Christ the Way, ### <-

Through faith and trust, the believer must look upon Christ as He hangs upon the cross, overcoming death through Death and defeating him who had the power of Death—the **Devil**—and so, in a meritorious way, obtaining this deliverance from the slavery of sin and of **Satan**. This is specifically a deliverance from the slavery of that *body of death* <note>A biblical reference to the corrupt nature of man, as seen in Romans 7:24.</note> and from the law of sin and of Death. For the Apostle tells us in *Romans 8: verse 2* that the Law of the Spirit of life in **Christ Jesus** makes us free from the law of sin and of Death. And this is because—as he further says in verses 3 and 4—what was impossible for the law, because it was weakened by the flesh, **God** has accomplished by sending His own Son in the likeness of sinful flesh, and that for sin; He has condemned sin in the flesh, so that the righteousness of the law might be fulfilled in us. Thus, the believer may look upon that enemy, however terrifying he may appear, as condemned and killed in the Death of **Christ**. He, having paid the price of redemption, has purchased this freedom from the chains and shackles with which the believer was held in prison. So then, faith in **Jesus'** Death—which satisfies justice for the poor prisoner—can and must support and strengthen the hope and confidence of the believer that he shall obtain the victory.

Twelfthly: It will further confirm the hope and faith of the believer to look upon **Christ** hanging on the cross, overpowering and conquering this Arch-enemy as a **Public Person** <note>A theological term meaning a legal representative who acts on behalf of others, such as a head of a covenant.</note> representing the elect, who died in Him. In Him, they virtually <note>original: *virtueellijk*, meaning in power or effect rather than physical presence.</note> or effectively and legally overcame that **Jailer** and broke his chains. The soul now believing may—indeed, must—hold it to be true that in **Christ**, as it were, it dies on the cross and there overcomes all these spiritual enemies; "Even so," says the Apostle in *Romans 6: verse 11*, "consider yourselves to be truly dead to sin." From now on, a believer—even while he is still fighting—may consider himself a victor—
